BE-2 BODY ELECTRICAL – MULTIPLEX COMMUNICATION
BODY ELECTRICAL
MULTIPLEX COMMUNICATION
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2) On the new models, a multiplex communication in which many different ECUs are linked to a bus line
and data are mutually transmitted among the ECUs through the line is used.
(cid:2) On the new models, the CAN (Controller Area Network) is used among the ECUs as shown below.

BE-4 BODY ELECTRICAL– MULTIPLEX COMMUNICATION
(cid:1)BASIC OF MPX
1. General
(cid:2) In the conventional system, a parallel communication is used to exchange information between ECUs. To
transmit four pieces of information, for example, the parallel communication requires four
communication wires. In contrast, a multiplex communication is used on a serial communication, which
converts multiple pieces of information into serial communication data. Thus, they can be transmitted
through a single communication wire.

BODY ELECTRICAL– MULTIPLEX COMMUNICATION BE-5
(cid:2) Serial communication data consist of bits and frames. A bit is the basic unit that represents the amount of
information. A bit is represented by binary values “0” or “1”. A frame is a body of data that is transmitted
together. A frame contains a header that indicates the beginning, and an end message that indicates the end.
Frame
Data
m
Header End Message
o 240BE05
2. Network Style c
.
General
n
Based on a serial communication, various ECUs are connected on a network to exchange various pieces
g
of information. Such a system is called a “Multiplex Communication”. There are three styles of networks:
ring, star, and bus.
a
BE
Ring Style i
d
In this style of network, the ECUs are connected in a ring form. A feature of this style is that a signal that
r
is output by a transmitting ECU circles the ring and returns to its original ECU.

BE-6 BODY ELECTRICAL– MULTIPLEX COMMUNICATION
Star Style
This style is centered on a master ECU, which holds a central control function. The ECUs are connected
in a star shape. The ECUs cannot establish a communication with other ECUs without passing through the
master ECU.

a
In this style of network, all ECUs are connected to a single common communication wire. The transmitting
i
ECUs output signals through the common communication wire and the receiving ECUs input data through
d
the common communication wire.

3. Differences of CAN and Other Communication Systems
.
(cid:2) Currently, Toyota vehicles use three types o n f multiplex communication systems: the CAN (Controller
Area Network), BEAN (Body Electronics Area Network), and AVC-LAN (Audio Visual
g
Communication-Local Area Network).
(cid:2) The protocols, which are the rulesa for establishing a data communication, differ between the CAN, BEAN BE
and AVC-LAN. If the ECUs use different types of data such as a communication speed, communication
i
wire, and signals, they will be unable to understand each other. Therefore, protocols (rules) must be
d
established among them.
r
CAN BEAN AVC-LAN
Protocol a
(ISO Standard) (TOYOTA Original) (TOYOTA Original)
c 500 k bps*
Communication Speed Max. 10 k bps* Max. 17.8 k bps*
(Max. 1 M bps)
Communication Wire Twisted-pair Wire AV Single Wire Twisted-pair Wire
Differential Single Wire Differential
Drive Type
Voltage Drive Voltage Drive Voltage Drive
Data Length 1-8 Byte (Variable) 1-11 Byte (Variable) 0-32 Byte (Variable)
*: bps: abbreviation for “Bits Per Second”, indicating the number of bits that can be transmitted per second.
(cid:2) Although the BEAN and AVC-LAN have almost the same communication speed, the communication
speed of the CAN is much faster than the BEAN and AVC-LAN.
(cid:2) When the chassis control system uses BEAN with a slower communication speed than CAN, the slower
communication speed means that system control could possibly be delayed.
(cid:2) For this reason, the power train and chassis control system uses the CAN which has a fast communication
speed and can send and receive a large quantity of data at one time.

BE-8 BODY ELECTRICAL– MULTIPLEX COMMUNICATION
4. Communication Wire
A twisted-pair wire is used for the CAN and AVC-LAN communication. A single, AV (Automobile Vinyl)
wire is used for the BEAN communication.

Voltage Drive”, can reduce noise.

BODY ELECTRICAL– MULTIPLEX COMMUNICATION BE-9
(cid:1)CAN (Controller Area Network)
(cid:2) The CAN (Controller Area Network) is a serial data communication for a real time application. It is a
multiplex communication to be equipped for a vehicle and has the communication speed (500 kbps) and
function to detect malfunction.
(cid:2) The communication wire uses a twisted-pair wire. The bus line has a high line (2.5 V to 3.5 V of voltage
is applied) and a low line (1.5 V to 2.5 V of voltage is applied).
(cid:2) The CAN system in the new models is used to connect the following ECUs and parts via junction connector
No. 1 and No. 2.

BE-10 BODY ELECTRICAL– MULTIPLEX COMMUNICATION
(cid:1)DIAGNOSIS
If a malfunction occurs on the CAN communication line, the ECU that is connected to the CAN
communication line stores the DTCs (Diagnostic Trouble Codes) in its memory.
(cid:2) There are 2-digit DTCs and 5-digit DTCs for the CAN communication.
– The 5-digit DTCs can be read by connecting an intelligent tester II to the DLC3.
– The 2-digit DTCs can be read from the warning light on the combination meter by connecting the SST
(09843-18040) to TC and CG terminals of the DLC3.
For details, see the Yaris Repair Manual (Pub. No. RM00S0E).

BE-14 BODY ELECTRICAL– LIGHTING
(cid:1)MANUAL HEADLIGHT BEAM LEVEL CONTROL SYSTEM
(cid:2) The manual headlight beam level control system is newly provided as standard equipment on the Europe
models.
(cid:2) This system keeps the beam of the headlight adjusted to the appropriate level in accordance with the
number (weight) of passengers and volume of luggage. The headlight beam level can be adjusted from
0 to 5 by operating the headlight beam level control switch.

BODY ELECTRICAL– LIGHTING BE-15
(cid:1)DAYTIME RUNNING LIGHT SYSTEM
(cid:2) The daytime running light system is provided as standard equipment on the cold area specification S rank
LHD models for Europe that are produced by TMC, and is provided as optional equipment on the cold
area specification S rank LHD models produced by TMMF.
(cid:2) This system is designed to automatically activate the headlights (high beam) and taillights during the
daytime to keep the car highly visible to other vehicles.
(cid:2) This system is controlled by the daytime running light relay.
(cid:2) This system is enabled when the conditions given below are met:
1) The power source mode is IG ON.
2) Alternator L terminal signal input is on (Engine running condition).
3) Light control switch is off.

(cid:1)LIGHT REMINDER SYSTEM
The light reminder system is newly provided as standard equipment on all models.
When the power source mode is changed from the IG ON to ACC or OFF while the driver’s door is open with
the taillights turned on, this system warns the driver that the lights remain on by sounding the multi buzzer.
The multi buzzer is located inside the combination meter.

---

BE-16 BODY ELECTRICAL– LIGHTING
(cid:1)ILLUMINATED ENTRY SYSTEM
(cid:2) The illuminated entry system is provided as standard equipment on the Europe models and as optional
equipment for all other models.
(cid:2) This system is controlled by the main body ECU.
(cid:2) When any one of the following conditions is satisfied, the illuminated entry system turns on the room light
and engine switch illumination*1.
1) Any door is opened.
2) A door is unlocked through a driver door key operation or transmitter operation *2
3) The key is in any of the actuation areas*1.
4) All doors closed and the power source mode is changed from IG ON to OFF.
(cid:2) When any one of the following conditions is satisfied, these lights will fade out (in approximately 1 second).
1) After continuing to be on for approximately 15 second after all doors have been properly closed.
2) All doors are closed and locked.
3) All doors are closed and the power source mode is changed to ACC or IG ON.
(cid:2) A battery saving function is used to automatically turn the room light and engine switch illumination OFF
m
when the following condition occurs continuously for over 20 minutes: the key is not in the ignition key
cylinder or the power source mode is OFF when any door is either ajar or open.
*1:Models with Smart Entry & Start System o
*2:Models with Wireless Door Lock Remote Control System

BE-18 BODY ELECTRICAL– METER
METER
(cid:1)COMBINATION METER
1. General
The combination meter in the new models has the following features:
(cid:2) The combination meter is available as a digital display type or as an Optitron display type. It is located
at the upper center of the instrument panel to ensure its visibility.
(cid:2) VFDs (Vacuum Fluorescent Displays) are used for the digital display type speedometer, tachometer, and
fuel gauge.
(cid:2) The Optitron display type meter realizes excellent visibility through the use of smoke acrylic in the
protective panel, and LEDs (Light Emitting Diodes) that are very bright and have high contrast for
illuminating the indicator and dial.
(cid:2) A step-motor type speedometer is used for the Optitron display type mcombination meter. LCDs (Liquid
Crystal Displays) are used for the odometer, trip meter, and fuel gauge.
(cid:2) A fuel gauge which displays the amount of fuel remaining in the fuel tank with an 8-segment bar is used.
o
(cid:2) A meter ECU and multi buzzer are enclosed in the combination meter. This ECU maintains
communication with other ECUs through the CAN (Contrcoller Area Network).
(cid:2) An oil change reminder light has been established in the. combination meter, which will light or flash to
remind the driver to change the engine oil. (Modenls with the 1ND-TV engine only)
(cid:2) The multi-information display is provided as standard equipment in the combination meter on the Europe
g
models.

3. Construction
Digital Display Type
On the digital display, a speedometer, tachometer and fuel gauge with an enlarged virtual image is used.
In this speedometer, tachometer and fuel gauge, the image that is displayed on a compact display tube (VFD:
Vacuum Fluorescent Display) is reflected by a mirror, in witch the magnified image is displayed. This type
realizes excellent visibility because it is less susceptible to external light.

Odo/Trip Meter
(cid:1) The display is used for the odo/trip meter, multi-information display*1. The switching of the modes is

(cid:1) While the trip meter A or B is displayed, pressing the odo/trip selector/reset knob 0.8 seconds or longer
causes the driven distance displayed by the current trip mode to revert to 0.0 mile (or km/h). The trip
meter will resume measuring the distance at the moment the odo/trip selector/reset knob is released.
(cid:1) While the dimming mode is displayed, the brightness of the meter can be changed by pressing the
odo/trip selector/reset knob 1.0 second or longer.
The selected dimming mode becomes canceled if a battery terminal is disconnected. When the battery
terminal is reconnected, the dimming mode sets to 100% (in daytime) or 12.5% (in nighttime) brightness.

BODY ELECTRICAL– METER BE-23
Fuel Gauge
(cid:1)A fuel gauge which displays the amount of fuel remaining in the fuel tank with an 8-segment bar is used.
(cid:1)A remaining fuel amount indicator function is used to blink the No. 1 segment bar when the amount of
remaining fuel is lower than the predetermined value. Furthermore, there are two patterns of blink cycles
depending on the amount of remaining fuel to provide a more accurate remaining fuel warning.
(cid:1)The amount of the remaining fuel is calculated and displayed by the meter ECU. The meter ECU
calculates this based on the signal from the fuel sender gauge and the vehicle speed signal from the skid
control ECU in accordance with the vehicle conditions such as driving or stopped.
(cid:1)The remaining fuel amount indicator function is activated when the fuel amount data calculated by the
meter ECU indicates the amount of remaining fuel has dropped to 15% or less, and causes the No. 1
segment bar to blink in approximately 1.2-second cycles. When the amount of remaining fuel drops to
10% or less, the No. 1 segment bar blinks in approximately 0.6-second cycles.

5.Oil Change Reminder Light Control (1ND-TV Engine Model)
r
(cid:1)An oil change remainder light is used to the combination meter. It will light or flash to remind the driver
to change the engine oil, depending on one of the following signals: the accumulated mileage/kilometrage
c
or the soot volume calculated in the engine ECU.
(cid:1)After the engine oil has been changed, accumulated mileage/kilometrage and soot volume memorized in
the engine ECU should be reset. At this point, the accumulated mileage/kilometrage and soot volume are
reset to 0 and the cycle begins again.
(cid:1)For details on the conditions in which the oil change reminder light illuminates or flashes, or on the
procedure for resetting the light, see page EG-191 in the EG section.

Servo Motor (with Automatic Air Conditioner)
In contrast to the previous type that detects the position by way of a potentiometer voltage, the pulse pattern
type servomotor detects the relative position by way of the 2-bit on/off signals.
The forward and reverse revolutions of this motor are detected by way of two phases, A and B, which output
four types of patterns. The air conditioner ECU counts the number of pulse patterns in order to determine
the stopped position.

BE-42 BODY ELECTRICAL– AIR CONDITIONER
3. Condenser
General
The new model uses a sub-cool condenser. This is a multi-flow condenser consisting of two cooling
portions: a condensing portion and super-cooling portion, and a gas-liquid separator (modulator) all
integrated together. This condenser uses a sub-cool cycle for its cooling cycle system to ensure
heat-exchanging efficiency.
Sub-Cool Cycle
In the sub-cool cycle, after the refrigerant passes through the condensing portion of the condenser, both the
liquid refrigerant and the gaseous refrigerant that could not be liquefied are cooled again in the
super-cooling portion. Thus, the refrigerant is sent to the evaporator in an almost completely liquefied state.

NOTE: a
The point at which the air bubbles disappear in the refrigerant of the sub-cool cycle is lower than the proper
c
amount of refrigerant with which the system must be filled. Therefore, if the system is recharged with
refrigerant based on the point at which the air bubbles disappear, the amount of refrigerant would be
insufficient. As a result, the cooling performance of the system will be affected. If the system is overcharged
with refrigerant, this will also lead to a reduced performance.
For the proper method of verifying the amount of the refrigerant and to recharge the system with refrigerant,
see the Yaris Repair Manual (Pub. No. RM00S0E).

4. A/C Compressor
General
An A/C compressor is a continuously variable capacity type in which its capacity varies in accordance with
the cooling load of the air conditioner.
(cid:2) This compressor consists of the shaft, lug plate, piston, shoe, crank chamber, cylinder, and solenoid
control valve.
(cid:2) The solenoid control valve that adjusts the suction pressure so that the suction pressure can be controlled
as desired is provided.
(cid:2) An A/C pulley is used the plastic DL (Damper Limiter) type.
(cid:2) A rotary valve type is used to suction refrigerant gas into the cylinder.

BE-44 BODY ELECTRICAL– AIR CONDITIONER
(cid:2) When the solenoid control valve closes (solenoid coil is energized), a difference in pressure is created
and the pressure in the crank chamber decreases. Then, the pressure that is applied to the right side of
the piston becomes greater than the pressure that is applied to the left side of the piston. This compresses
the spring and tilts the lug plate. As a result, the piston stroke increases and the discharge capacity
increases.

BODY ELECTRICAL– AIR CONDITIONER BE-47
2. Neural Network Control
(cid:2) In the previous automatic air conditioner system, the ECU determined the required outlet air temperature
and blower air volume in accordance with the calculation formula that has been obtained based on
information received from the sensors.
However, because the sensors of a person are rather complex, a given temperature is sensed differently,
depending on the environment in which the person is situated. For example, a given amount of solar
radiation can feel comfortably warm in a cold climate, or extremely uncomfortable in a hot climate.
Therefore, as a technique for effecting a high level of control, a neural network has been used in the
automatic air conditioner system. With this technique, the data that has been collected under varying
environmental conditions is stored in the ECU, which effects control to provide enhanced air conditioner
comfort.
(cid:2) The neural network control consists of neurons in the input layer, intermediate layer, and output layer. The
input layer neurons process the input data of the outside temperature, the amount of sunlight, and the room
temperature based on the outputs of the switches and sensors, and output them to the intermediate layer
m
neurons. Based on this data, the intermediate layer neurons adjust the strength of the links among the
neurons. The sum of these is then calculated by the output layer neurons in the form of the required outlet
temperature, solar correction, target airflow volume, ando outlet mode control volume. Accordingly, the
air conditioner ECU controls the servo motors and blower motor in accordance with the control volumes
c
that have been calculated by the neural network control.

3. Self-Diagnosis
The air conditioner ECU has a self-diagnosis function. It stores any operation failures in the air conditioner
system memory in the form of DTC (Diagnostic Trouble Code).
For details, see the Yaris Repair Manual (Pub. No. RM00S0E).

BE-48 BODY ELECTRICAL– AIR CONDITIONER
(cid:3)COMBUSTION TYPE POWER HEATER
1. General
(cid:2) While the 1ND-TV engine offers excellent combustion efficiency, it generates less heat. Therefore,
depending on the operating condition, the coolant temperature remains low, making it difficult to ensure
the proper heating effect. Thus, in order to provide adequate heating effect, a combustion type power
heater is provided as optional equipment on the LHD models with the cold area specification for Europe.
(cid:2) This heater system provides a combustion type power heater between the engine and the heater core. Air
and fuel are introduced into the heater’s combustion chamber, and a glow plug is used to ignite this
mixture. By heating the engine coolant that flows around the combustion chamber, this system ensures
the heating effect. The system can be activated or deactivated by turning the power heater switch provided
on the floor console on or off.

BE-50 BODY ELECTRICAL– AIR CONDITIONER
4. Combustion Type Power Heater Assembly
General
The combustion type power heater assembly consists mainly of a heater exchanger, glow plug, motor, air
fan, ECU, water temperature sensor, overheating prevention sensor, flame sensor, intake pipe, exhaust pipe
and fuel pump.
This heater is installed between the engine and heater core. The coolant from the engine flows through the
spiral passage around the heat exchanger and flows into the heater core. At this time, the glow plug ignites
the air and fuel in the combustion chamber of the heat exchanger, and the resultant heat of combustion heats
the engine coolant.

BODY ELECTRICAL– AIR CONDITIONER BE-51
5. Operation
General
1) Igniting
When the engine is operating, turning the power switch on causes the air fan to operate for several seconds
for verification purposes. Then, the glow plug starts to preheat the combustion chamber. After that, the fuel
pump and air fan turn on in order to start low combustion. The fuel pump speed is then increased in steps,
and this is accompanied by a gradual increase in the speed of the air fan, thus leading to high combustion.
2) Extinguishing
When ignition switch is turned off or the power heater switch is turned off, the fuel pump stops, causing
the combustion to stop. For the purpose of after-purge, current is applied again to the glow plug, and the
air fan is activated for several seconds. Then, the entire systemm comes to stop.

BE-52 BODY ELECTRICAL– AIR CONDITIONER
Protective Control
For self-protection, this system stops if an abnormal condition is detected. Descriptions of the control are
indicated below.
Function Control
If the temperature detected by the water temperature sensor or the
overheating prevention sensor exceeds 125(cid:1)C, the ECU determines
Dry run prevention
that the heater is operating without engine coolant and automatically
stops the system.
If the difference in temperature detected by the water temperature
sensor and the overheating prevention sensor exceeds 15(cid:1)C, the ECU
Overheating prevention
determines that the flow volume of the engine coolant is insufficient
and automatically stops the system.
If the temperature of the exhaust gasm detected by the flame sensor is
Non-ignition or misfiring
low, the ECU determines that a non-ignition or a misfiring condition
detection
exists and automatically stops the system.
o
If an open or short circuit exists in the sensors or actuators, the ECU
Open or short circuit detection
automatically stops the sycstem.
Air fan seizure detection If the air fan becomes seized, the ECU automatically stops the system.
.
n
Diagnosis
g
If a malfunction occurs in the system, it is possible to access the diagnostic trouble code by using a SST
a
(Special Service Tools) that has been designed exclusively for the combustion type power heater. For details
on the diagnostic trouble codes, etc., described above, refer to the Yaris Repair Manual (Pub. No.
i
RM00S0E). d
r
NOTES:
(cid:2) When the power heater a is turned on or off, some white smoke and a slight odor may be emitted from the
exhaust located under the floor. If the power heater is being used under extremely cold conditions, vapor
c
may be visible from the exhaust. This is normal.
(cid:2) We recommend not to restart the power heater for 10 minutes after you turn it off.
Otherwise, a noise may be heard as the heater ignites.
(cid:2) Do not turn the power heater on and off repeatedly within 5 minutes intervals as this can shorten the life
of the heater components. If the engine is to be turned on and off repeatedly within short intervals (such
as when being used for delivery purposes), turn the power heater switch off.

BE-56 BODY ELECTRICAL– POWER WINDOW SYSTEM
(cid:3)JAM PROTECTION FUNCTION
1. General
(cid:2) The jam protection function automatically stops the power window and moves it downward if foreign
object gets jammed in the door window during one-touch auto-up operation or manual-up operation.
(cid:2) The mechanism uses two Hall ICs to detect the door glass position and direction of movement.
(cid:2) The power window ECU is built into the power window motor assembly.
(cid:2) The reverse-direction conditions and amount of down movement after jam detection are as follows: The
jam protection function operates and lowers the window approximately 50 mm or until 1 second has
elapsed. After this, if the amount (space) that the door glass is open has not reached approximately 200
mm, the jam protection function will continue to lower the window until the space is approximately 200
mm or 5 seconds has elapsed. If the door glass reaches the fully open position before the initial 50 mm
lowering of the door glass is reached, the jam protection function will stop at that time.
m
2. Jammed Window Detection Mechanism
(cid:2) The jammed window detection mechanism consists of a magnet t o hat is provided on the worm gear of the
power window motor assembly, the power window ECU and the two Hall ICs that are provided on the
c
connector.
.
n

Power Window Motor
gMagnet
Connector Portion a
i
d
r

BODY ELECTRICAL– POWER WINDOW SYSTEM BE-57
(cid:2) The Hall IC converts the changes in the magnetic flux that occur through the rotation of the worm gear
into block wave signals and outputs them to the power window ECU.
(cid:2) To control the jam protection function, the power window ECU determines the amount of movement and
the jamming of the window glass from the pulse signals from the Hall IC 1, and the moving direction of
the window glass from the phase difference between the pulses from the Hall IC 1 and Hall IC 2.

Service Tip
If the power window motor assembly and power window regulator assembly are replaced, the
window position information stored in the system will be lost, and the system must be initialized.
After replacing the above parts, initialize the system by performing the steps described below.
1)Set the power source mode to IG ON, and fully open the driver’s window. Continue to hold down
the switch for at least 1 second after the window has fully opened.
2)Hold the AUTO switch in the auto up position. Continue to hold the AUTO switch in the auto up
position for approximately 1 second after the window has fully closed. The system will memorize
the window’s fully closed position as the initial position.
For details, see the Yaris Repair Manual (Pub. No. RM00S0E).
*: Even if the window was already in the fully open position, the switch must still be held down for
approximately 1 second.

BE-58 BODY ELECTRICAL– DOOR LOCK CONTROL SYSTEM
DOOR LOCK CONTROL SYSTEM
(cid:3)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2)The door lock control system is provided as standard equipment on the Europe models and is provided
as optional equipment on all other models.
(cid:2)This system is controlled by the main body ECU. The main body ECU outputs signals to the respective
door lock motors.
(cid:2)The door lock system in the new models has the following functions:
Function Outline
This function can lock or unlock all doors by the door lock control switch
Manual Lock and Unlock
operation.
Key-linked Lock and This function which is linked with the key cylinder can lock or unlock
Unlock*1 all the doors when a lock or unlock operation is effected.
m
When the key is inserted in the ignition key cylinder with the driver’s
Key Confine Prevention door open and if you perform the door lock operation, all the doors will
be unlocked. o
Performing the door lock operation with a transmitter or an ignition key
Manual Unlock Prohibition c
will prohibit the unlock operation by the door lock control switch.
.
(cid:2)The double locking system also prevents the doors from being opened
n
through the operation of the inside door knobs. Thus, the doors cannot
be opened egither from the inside or the outside of the vehicle.
(cid:2)To lock the doors with this system, pressing the transmitter’s lock
buttona, then pressing it again within 5 seconds activates the double
locking system. However, as an emergency unlocking maneuver,
i
Double Locking System*2 only the driver’s door can be unlocked with a key.
d
rCAUTION:
aNever activate the double locking system when there are people in the
vehicle because the doors cannot be opened from the inside of the
c
vehicle.
If locking the doors by accident, press “unlock” button of the transmitter.
With this function, the user can unlock the driver’s or front passenger’s
Entry Unlock Function*3 door by keeping a key in his or her possession and touching the outside
door handle.
With this function, the user can lock all doors by keeping a key in his or
Entry Lock Function*3 her possession and pressing the lock button that is provided on the
outside door handle.
Door Lock with Transmitter
Pressing the door lock button of the transmitter locks all doors.
Function (see page BE-63)
Door Unlock with Transmitter
Pressing the door unlock button of the transmitter unlocks all doors.
Function (see page BE-63)
*1:On the models with the double locking system, when the double locking system is operating, all doors
can be locked through the key-linked function, but they cannot be unlocked. Only the driver’s door can
be unlocked by the key.
*2:This system is optional equipment on the RHD models for Europe
*3:Models with Smart Entry & Start System

BODY ELECTRICAL – WIRELESS DOOR LOCK REMOTE CONTROL SYSTEM BE-61
WIRELESS DOOR LOCK REMOTE CONTROL SYSTEM
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
The wireless door lock remote control system is provided as standard equipment on the SOL grade for the
Europe models and the models with the smart entry & start system, and is provided as optional equipment
on all other models.
This system is a convenient system for locking and unlocking all doors at a distance. This system in the new
models has the following features:
(cid:2) In this system, the wireless door lock receiver performs the code identification process and sends the lock
or unlock signal to the main body ECU, then the main body ECU effects the door lock control.
(cid:2) A key-integrated, two-button (lock, unlock) type transmitter is used.
(cid:2) The hazard warning light blinks when the door lock or unlock operation is performed by the wireless door
lock remote control system.

SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
The smart entry & start system has a wireless door lock remote control function and engine immobilizer
function. Furthermore, in this system, the following functions (entry function and start function) are also
possible simply by carrying the key in the driver’s possessions without using a key or transmitter button.
(cid:2) Door unlock/lock (entry unlock/entry lock functions)
(cid:2) Engine start simply by pressing the engine switch while depressing the brake pedal*1 or clutch pedal*2
(start function)
(cid:2) Back door open (entry back door open function)
This system is optional equipment on the SOL grade models for Europe.

BE-66 BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM
(cid:1)START FUNCTION
1.General
(cid:2)On the models without the smart entry & start system, the ignition key is inserted into the key cylinder
and turned to switch the ignition switch position from the OFF position to the ACC, IG ON or START
position.
(cid:2)On the models with the smart entry & start system, it is operated simply by pressing the push-type engine
switch while carrying the key. The main body ECU turns OFF and ON the ACC, IG1, IG2 or ST CUT
relays to switch the power source mode.
(cid:2)This function has different power source mode patterns to suit the brake pedal condition, shift lever
position and clutch pedal condition. For details, see page BE-74.
(cid:2) Along with the adoption of the start function, an engine cranking hold function is used.

6. Construction and Operation
Engine Switch
The engine switch consists of the momentary type switch, two color (amber, green) LEDs, and transponder
key amplifier.
(cid:2) The amber and green LEDs are for the indicator light.
(cid:2) The driver can check the present power source mode and whether the engine can start or not in accordance
with the illumination state of the indicator light.
(cid:2) When the main body ECU detects an abnormality in the start system while the engine is running, it makes
the amber indicator light flash. If the engine stopped in this state, it might not be possible to restart it.

BE-72 BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM
Main Body ECU
The main body ECU consists of the IG1 and IG2 relay actuation circuits, CPU, and hold circuit.
(cid:2) The hold circuit is installed to prevent the power supply to the relays from being cut off when an
abnormality occurs in IG1 and/or IG2 relay actuation circuits during driving.

Service Tip
c
The main body ECU constantly stores the present power source mode in its memory. Therefore, if
the power to the main body ECU is interrupted due to the removal of the battery, the main body ECU
restores the power source mode after the battery is reconnected.
For this reason, if the battery is removed when the engine switch is in the mode other than OFF, the
power source mode will be restored to the vehicle at the same time the power source mode is restored
to the main body ECU (by reconnecting the battery).
Therefore, before remove or disconnect the battery, make sure to turn the engine switch off.

BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM BE-73
Steering Lock System
According with the use of the smart entry & start system, a steering lock system which uses a lock/unlock
motor to lock and unlock the steering wheel is used. This system mainly consists of the steering lock
assembly, main body ECU, certification ECU and ID code box.
(cid:2) The steering lock ECU is integrated in the steering lock assembly, and it controls the lock bar operation
in the steering lock assembly through the control of the lock/unlock motor.
(cid:2) The steering lock ECU detects the position (lock/unlock) of the lock bar and transmits this information
to the main body ECU and certification ECU.
(cid:2) In this system, the certification ECU determines whether to lock or unlock the steering wheel based on
communication with the main body ECU. Then, the certification ECU sends the lock or unlock command
signal to the steering lock ECU through the ID code box. Upon receiving the signal, the steering lock
ECU operates the lock/unlock motor to lock or unlock the steering wheel.

Service Tip
It is not possible to replace only the steering lock ECU in the steering lock assembly. Therefore, if
a malfunction occurs in the ECU, the entire steering lock assembly must be replaced.
Service Tip
After recharging a depleted battery, the engine may not start the first time (due to the steering lock
ECU not correctly detecting the lock bar position).
When this happens, turn off all vehicle power supplies (ACC=OFF, IG=OFF), open and close the
doors, and then start the engine again.

When the engine ECU judges from the engine speed signal that engine start is completed, it

The main body ECU checks that engine start is completed, and turns OFF the indicator light

BODY ELECTRICAL – SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM BE-81
When key battery is low
Step System Operation
To operate the start system when the key battery is low, hold the TOYOTA mark of the key
a)
against the engine switch while depressing the brake pedal.
b) The main body ECU transmits a key certification request signal to the certification ECU.
The certification ECU does not receive an ID code response from the tuner, so it actuates the
c)
transponder key amplifier built into the engine switch.
d) The transponder key amplifier outputs an engine immobilizer radio wave to the key.
The key receives the radio wave, and returns a radio wave response to the transponder key
e)
amplifier.
The transponder key amplifier combines the key ID codes with the radio wave response, and
f)
transmits it to the certification ECU.
The certification ECU judges and verifies the ID code, and transmits a key certification OK
g) m
signal to the main body ECU. The buzzer in the combination meter sounds at the same time.
After the buzzer sounds, if the engine switch is pressed within five seconds while the brake
h) pedal*1 or clutch pedal*2 is depressed, the powero source mode switches to start as in the normal
condition.

i) pedal*1 or clutch pedal2* is not depressed, the power source mode switches to ACC or IG-ON

BE-82 BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM
8. Diagnosis
The main body ECU can detect malfunctions in the start function when the power source mode is IG-ON.
When the ECU detects a malfunction, the amber indicator light of the engine switch flashes to warn the
driver. At the same time, the ECU stores the 5-digit DTC (Diagnostic Trouble Code) in the memory.
(cid:1) The indicator light warning continues for 15 seconds even when the power source mode is switched to
OFF.
(cid:1) The DTC can be read by connecting an intelligent tester II to the DLC3.
(cid:1) The start function cannot be operated again if a malfunction occurs.
For details, see the Yaris Repair Manual (Pub. No. RM00S0E).

BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM BE-87
4. Construction and Operation
Key
The key consists of a mechanical key, transmitter for the wireless door lock remote control, transceiver for
the smart entry & start system, and transponder chip for the engine immobilizer control.
(cid:1) The transceiver for the smart entry & start system receives the signals from the oscillators and returns
the ID code to the tuner.
(cid:1) The transmitter for the wireless door lock remote control has a LOCK button and UNLOCK button.
(cid:1) This mechanical key works for the driver door, but cannot start the engine.

Oscillator (Front LH & RH Doors, Front and Rear Rooms and Back Door)
r
Each oscillator transmits the request signal received from the certification ECU, and forms a key actuation
a
area to detect the presence of a key.
The actuation area formed by front LH & RH door oscillators and back door outer oscillator are
c
approximately 0.7 to 1.0 m (2.3 to 3.3 ft.) from the outside handle of the front LH & RH doors, or the center
of the rear bumper.
(cid:1) The actuation area of the front LH & RH door oscillators are formed by transmitting a request signal
every 250 ms while the engine switch Is off And each door is locked. In this way it detects the proximity
of a key. During entry lock, the actuation area is formed with the lock switch on.
(cid:1) The actuation area of the back door outer oscillator is formed when the back door open Switch Is on. It
is formed twice until the key can be verified.
(cid:1) The actuation area of the front and rear room oscillators are formed when the driver door is opened or
closed, during start ignition, when a warning is activated, or when the lock switch is on.

BE-88 BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM
5.Entry Function Operation
General
The entry function has the following functions.
Function Outline
This function is a convenient system for locking and unlocking all the doors
or back door at a distance. The operation of this function in the smart entry
Wireless Door Lock & start system is the same as that in the wireless door lock remote control
Remote Control system.
For details, see page BE-61. However, the receiver in the certification ECU
uses a tuner to perform the control.
Entry Unlock When a key is located in any actuation area of the door oscillator, the door
[See page BE-89] will be unlocked with the touch of an outside door handle.
m
When a key is located in any actuation area of the door oscillator and the
Entry Lock
power source mode is OFF, the door will be locked by merely pressing the
[See page BE-90]
lock switch on the outside door handole.
Entry Back Door Open When a key is in an actuation area of the back door outer oscillator, the back
c
[See page BE-91] door opens by merely pressing the back door open switch.
(cid:1)Prevents the confinement of . the key if the door is locked from the outside

Confinement (cid:1)If the back door isg closed while the key is still in the luggage compartment,
[See page BE-92] the warning buzzer sounds. If the back door open switch is operated in two
seconds, thae back door can be opened.
The smart entry & start system causes the certification ECU to sound the
i
Warning buzzedr in the combination meter and the wireless door lock buzzer, and

BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM BE-89
Entry Unlock
a) When a key enters any actuation area of the door oscillators, the certification ECU judges and certifies
the key ID code received from the tuner.
b) After the key ID is certified, the certification ECU transmits an unlock stand-by signal to the touch sensor
of the relevant door.
c) At the same time, the certification ECU transmits a lighting signal for each illumination (engine switch
illumination and room light) via the main body ECU, and turns on these illuminations. (Entry
Illumination Function)
d) If the touch sensor is touched in this condition, the certification ECU transmits a door unlock request
signal to the main body ECU, and unlocks the door.

BE-90 BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM
Entry Lock
a) The lock signal is transmitted to the certification ECU when the driver exits the vehicle with the key
carried in the driver’s possessions and presses the lock switch.
b) The certification ECU transmits a request signal for all the door and room oscillators to form actuation
areas.
c) The key transmits the ID code to the tuner.
d) The certification ECU judges and certifies the ID code from the tuner. It then checks the location of the
key, and when all the doors are closed, the ECU transmits a door lock request signal to the main body
ECU.
e) The main body ECU actuates the door lock motors to lock the doors.

Entry Back Door Open
a) The unlock signal is transmitted to the certification ECU when the driver pushes the back door open
switch with the key carried in the driver’s possessions.
b) The certification ECU transmits a request signal for all the room and back door outer oscillators to form
actuation areas.
c) The key transmits the ID code to the tuner.
d) The certification ECU judges and certifies the ID code, and checks the location of the key. The ECU
transmits a back door open signal to the main body ECU.
e) The main body ECU receives this signal and actuates the back door opener motor to open the back door.

BE-92 BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM
Prevention of Key Confinement
1) General
This function has two system operations: inside room and inside luggage compartment.
2) Inside Room
a) When the door is locked from the lock switch while the key is still inside the vehicle, the certification
ECU transmits a request signal for the front and rear room oscillators to form a actuation area.
b) The key transmits the ID code to the tuner.
c) The certification ECU judges and certifies the ID code, and checks the location of the key. The ECU
transmits a door unlock request signal to the main body ECU.
d) The main body ECU receives the signal and operates each door lock motor to unlock the doors.
e) The certification ECU sounds the wireless door lock buzzer and the buzzer in the combination meter
m
as a response to unlock.

BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM BE-93
3) Inside Luggage Room
a) When the back door is closed while the key is still inside the luggage room, the certification ECU
recognizes the back door close condition from the main body ECU.
b) The certification ECU transmits a request signal for the rear room oscillator to form an actuation area.
c) The key transmits the ID code to the tuner.
d) The certification ECU judges and certifies the ID code, and checks the location of the key. The ECU
sounds the wireless door lock buzzer for two seconds to inform the driver.
e) If the back door open switch is turned on while the key is inside the luggage room, the certification
ECU sends another request signal for the rear room oscillator to form an actuation area. The ECU
judges and certifies the key ID and checks its position before transmitting a back door open request
signal to the main body ECU.
f) The main body ECU receives the signal and operates the back door opener motor to open the back
door. m

BE-94 BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM
Warning
1) General
When any of the situations below occur, the smart entry & start system causes the certification ECU to
sound a buzzer in the combination meter and the wireless door lock buzzer, and illuminate the smart entry
& start system warning light in order to alert the driver.
Situation Condition
A The driver door is opened while the steering is unlocked.
B The entry lock is operated while any of the doors is open.
C The occupant leaves with the key.
D The engine switch is operated while the key is outside the actuation area.
E The entry lock is operated while the key is inside the vehicle.
F The key battery is low.
G The steering lock cannot be released.
m
H A steering lock ECU malfunction has been detected.
I A main body ECU malfunction has been detected.
o
J
A clutch start switch malfunction has been detected.

BODY ELECTRICAL– SMART ENTRY & START SYSTEM BE-97
Battery Saving
1) Vehicle Battery Saving Function
In the smart entry & start system, signals are emitted outside of the vehicle at a prescribed interval (250
ms) when the doors are locked. Therefore, the vehicle battery could be drained if the vehicle remains
parked for a long time. For this reason, the controls listed below are effected.
Condition Control
No response from key for
Signal transmission interval is extended from 250 ms to 750 ms.
more than 5 days
No response from key for
Automatically deactivates the smart entry & start system.
more than 14 days
(cid:3) Reinstatement Conditions (cid:4) m
(cid:1) A wireless door lock remote control signal (lock, unlock, or back door open) is input and the key ID
matches. o
(cid:1) A driver carries the key and pushes the lock switch of the outside door handle.
c
(cid:1) A door is locked or unlocked by the mechanical key.
.
n
2) Key Battery and Vehicle Battery Saving Function
g
In the smart entry & start system, if the key is constantly located within the vehicle exterior actuation
area of the door oscillators, the system maintains periodic communication with the key. Therefore, if the
a
BE
vehicle remains parked in that state for a long time, the key battery and the vehicle battery could be
drained. i
d
For this reason, if this state continues longer than 10 minutes, the smart entry & start system automatically
becomes deactivated.
r
a
(cid:3) Reinstatement Conditions (cid:4)
c
(cid:1) A wireless door lock remote control signal (lock, unlock, or back door open) is input and the key ID
matches.
(cid:1) A driver carries the key and pushes the lock switch of the outside door handle.
(cid:1) A door is locked or unlocked by the mechanical key.
Service Tip
Leaving the key close to electrical appliances that emit radio waves could cause the key to
accidentally respond, which could cause the key battery to become quickly depleted.

BE-98 BODY ELECTRICAL– ENGINE IMMOBILIZER SYSTEM
ENGINE IMMOBILIZER SYSTEM
(cid:2)DESCRIPTION
The engine immobilizer system compares the ID code that is registered in the transponder key ECU with the
ID code of the transponder chip that is embedded in the ignition key. The system unsets if these ID codes
match. Thus, the transponder key ECU and engine ECU communicate with each other to authorize fuel
injection and ignition, enabling the engine to start.
(cid:1)The system is standard equipment on the models (except the general countries models) without the smart
entry & start system.
(cid:1)The engine immobilizer function is provided on the models with the smart entry & start system. For details,
see page BE-64.
Service Tip
When replacing the transponder key ECU or adding a new ignition kmey, the key’s recognition code
must be registered.
(cid:1)On the Europe models, when the transponder key ECU has been replaced, the intelligent tester II
o
is necessary for the key registration. At this time, the total number of keys that can be registered
is two (master key: two). c
(cid:1)On the models other than the Europe models, when the transponder key ECU has been replaced,
.
the automatic registration mode begins. At this time, the total number of keys that can be registered
n
is three (master key: two, sub key: one).
(cid:1)The recognition code of additional keys must be registered. At this time, the total number of keys
g
that can be registered is eight (master key: five, sub key: three).
For details, see the Yaris Repair Manaual (Pub. No. RM00S0E).

BODY ELECTRICAL– SRS AIRBAG SYSTEM
BE
epyT
gabriA
BE-99
SRS AIRBAG SYSTEM
(cid:2)DESCRIPTION
(cid:1) The SRS (Supplemental Restraint System) driver and front passenger airbags are used to help reduce the
shocks to the heads and chests of the driver and front passenger in the event of frontal impact collision as
supplements to the seat belts. The SRS airbag system controls the airbag inflating output by judging the
extent of impact.
(cid:1) To supplement the seat belts, an SRS knee airbag has been newly designed to help ensure good protection
of the driver in the event of a frontal collision.
(cid:1) An airbag manual ON-OFF switch which disables the deployment of the front passenger airbag and the
front passenger seat side airbag as deemed necessary is provided as standard equipment on the Europe
models.
(cid:1) The SRS side and curtain shield airbags are used to help reduce the shocks to the heads and chests of the
driver, front passenger, and outer rear passengers in the event of a side collision.
m
(cid:1) As a countermeasure against an interruption in the power supply in the event of a collision involving the
vehicle, a backup power supply which consists of a power supply capacitor and a voltage boost circuit
o
(DC-DC Converter) is provided in the airbag sensor assembly.
(cid:1) The airbag sensor assembly uses a fuel cut control thact stops the fuel pump if any airbag is deployed (only
on the gasoline engine models).
.
(cid:1) The new Yaris has the SRS airbag system wnith the following equipments:

BE-104 BODY ELECTRICAL– SRS AIRBAG SYSTEM
4. Airbag Manual ON-OFF Switch (Only for Europe models)
(cid:1) A switch to disable the deployment of the front passenger airbag and the front passenger seat side airbag
has been provided as standard equipment. This switch can be used if the deployment of those airbags is
inconvenient, such as when the passenger is wearing a CRS seat belt.
(cid:1) This switch is located on the side surface of the front passenger side of the instrument panel. It is operated
on or off by a mechanical key, which is supplied with the wireless key. Once the airbag manual ON-OFF
switch is turned to OFF, the front passenger airbag and the front passenger seat side airbag will be disabled,
regardless of the collision conditions.
(cid:1) If the deployment of the front passenger airbag has been disabled, the passenger airbag OFF indicator light
in the center cluster illuminates to inform the occupant.

BODY ELECTRICAL– SRS AIRBAG SYSTEM BE-105
(cid:2)AIRBAG FOR SIDE/REAR SIDE COLLISION
1. General
The airbag for side collisions contains two airbags: side and curtain shield. These airbags deploy
simultaneously. The airbag for rear side collisions contains only the curtain shield airbag.
(cid:1) With the airbag for side collisions, if the side airbag sensor detects an impact, it informs the airbag sensor
assembly, and the airbag sensor assembly causes the side and curtain shield airbags to be deployed
simultaneously.
(cid:1) With the airbag for rear side collisions, if the curtain shield airbag sensor detects an impact, it informs the
airbag sensor assembly via the side airbag sensor, and the airbag sensor assembly causes the curtain shield
airbag to be deployed.

(cid:2)DIAGNOSIS
1. General
If the airbag sensor assembly detects a malfunction in the SRS airbag system, the airbag sensor assembly
stores the malfunction data in memory, in addition to illuminating the SRS warning light.
(cid:1) The airbag sensor assembly outputs malfunction data, 5-digit DTCs (Diagnostic Trouble Codes) or 2-digit
DTCs, to the intelligent tester II or the SRS warning light.

2. DTC of SRS Airbag System
There are 2 types of DTCs of the SRS airgbag system: 5-digit and 2-digit.
(cid:1) The 5-digit DTC can be read by connecting an Intelligent Tester II to the DLC3 terminal.
a

(cid:1) The 2-digit DTC can be read through the blinking of the SRS warning light with the SST(09843-18040)

connected between the TC and CG terminals of the DLC3.

different from the ordinary diagnosis function that a DTC will not be memorized. The SRS warning light
can be turned offa only by changing the airbag sensor assembly to a new one.

(cid:1) Seat heaters are provided as optional equipment in the driver seat and front passenger seat on the cold area

(cid:1) On the previous model, the seat cushion heater was integrated into the seat cushion pad, but on the new

BODY ELECTRICAL– SEAT BELT REMINDER SYSTEM BE-109
SEAT BELT REMINDER SYSTEM
(cid:2)DESCRIPTION
If the seat belt is not buckled, this system flashes the seat belt reminder light and sounds the multi buzzer as
a reminder.
(cid:1) A seat belt reminder system is provided as standard equipment on all models except General Countries
models.
(cid:1) When the power source mode is IG ON, this system detects the condition of the seat belts based on the
signals from the seat belt buckle switches (for the driver and front passenger) and the occupant detection
sensor.
(cid:1) The buzzer sounds when the vehicle speed exceeds 20 km/h (12.4 mph) to emphasize the fact that the seat
belt is unbuckled. After 30 seconds, this sound changed to emphasize. This sound sounds during 90
seconds. Once a buzzer begins to sound, it will continue until the seat belt is buckeled or the engine switch
is turned off.

BE-110 BODY ELECTRICAL– SEAT BELT REMINDER SYSTEM
(cid:2)OCCUPANT DETECTION SENSOR
The occupant detection sensor, which is enclosed in the seat cushion of the front passenger seat, is used to
detect whether or not the front passenger seat is occupied.
This sensor, which is shaped as illustrated below, consists of a construction in which two electrode sheets
sandwich a spacer. When the occupant is seated, the electrode sheets come in contact with each other through
the hole that is provided in the spacer portion, thus enabling the current to flow.
Thus, the sensor detects whether or not an occupant is seated in the front passenger seat.

1. General
The impact absorbing structure of the new Yaris can effectively help reduce the impact in the event of a front
or side collision. This structure also realizes high-performance occupant protection through the use of
reinforcement and member that help minimize cabin deformation.

effectively dissipate the energy of the impact applied to the front of the vehicle: the front bumper
reinforcements, under-floor reinforcements, dash panel reinforcements, floor tunnel reinforcements,
rockers, front pillars, and door inner reinforcements.

easily corroded parts such as the hood, doors and rocker panels.

CH-2 CHASSIS – CLUTCH
CHASSIS
CLUTCH
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
New Yaris has a clutch system with the following features.
(cid:2)A dry type single plate clutch which is operated by hydraulic pressure is used.
(cid:2)A clutch master cylinder made of plastic is used.
(cid:2)To prevent the transaxle from making gear lash noise as a result of a fluctuation in engine speed, the clutch
disc is provided with a pre-damper for the TMMF models.
(cid:2)A clutch pedal made of plastic is used on the LHD models.
(cid:2)The clutch systems of C53A and C551A multi-mode manual transmission models have following
features.
m
Outline
(cid:2)A clutch disc that uses lead-free material is used to help protect the environment.
(cid:2)Instead of a hydraulic type clutch system, a clutch actuator that op o erates the clutch electrically is used.
For details, see page CH-20.
c
(cid:2)An LCC (Load Controlled Clutch cover) mechanism is used in the clutch cover to mechanically adjust
the pressure plate height which changes with the wear o.f the clutch disc facing. For details, see page
n
CH-22.

CH-4 CHASSIS– CLUTCH
(cid:1)PRE-DAMPER
A pre-damper, consisting of independent inner and outer hubs and coil springs placed on the inner hub, is
positioned in the center of the clutch disc. It absorbs the vibration that is generated by fluctuations in engine
speed during idling and therefore reduces the gear lash noise created in the transaxle. The main damper spring
carries out the same vibration reduction function during normal driving.

CH-6 CHASSIS– MANUAL TRANSAXLE
MANUAL TRANSAXLE
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2)The new model uses 3 types (C53, C54, C551) of manual transaxle.
(cid:2)C53A multi-mode manual transmission and C551A multi-mode manual transmission are provided on the
TMMF models. For details on the multi-mode manual transmission system, see page CH-10.

shift operations.

the mass damper on the shift and select shaft, and sliding ball bearing.
(cid:2) A shift guide plate is provided on the shift and select shaft. The movement distance of the shift and select
shaft in the select direction after the shifting is completed is regulated by the shift guide plate and shift
guide pin. This clarifies the position of the shift knob and enables accurate shift operations.

CH-10 CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION
MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION
(cid:1)C53A AND C551A MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION SYSTEMS
1. General
(cid:2) C53A multi-mode manual transmission is used on the 1ND-TV engine model.
(cid:2) C551A multi-mode manual transmission is used on the 1KR-FE and 2SZ-FE engine models.
(cid:2) The multi-mode manual transmission system consists of two types of shift mode: the E-mode that shifts
automatically to suit the driving conditions, and the M-mode that allows the driver to manually operate
the shift lever to shift gears without applying the clutch. As a result, good fuel efficiency, ease of driving
(in the E-mode) and a sporty drive (in the M-mode) have been realized.
(cid:2) In the E-mode, the driver can select two shift patterns: the normal [E] pattern and sporty [Es] pattern.
(cid:2) This system consists of C53A and C551A multi-mode manual transmissions, engine ECU, M-MT
(Multi-mode Manual Transmission) ECU, with LCC (Load Controlled Clutch cover), and a shift lever.
The C53A and C551A multi-mode manual transmissions are based on the C53 and C551 manual
m
transaxles, to which clutch actuator, shift & select actuator, transmission revolution sensor, and neutral
start switch have been added.
(cid:2) The basic constructions and main operations of the multi-mode moanual transmission system for the new
model are same as those on the previous model (Pub. No. NCF244E).

CH-16 CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION
(cid:1)CONSTRUCTION AND OPERATION
1. Shift & Select Actuator
General
(cid:2) The shift & select actuator consists of a shift motor, select motor, shift stroke sensor, select stroke sensor,
and shift & select mechanism (shift & select inner lever, shift & select lever shaft, shift motor shaft, select
motor shaft, ring gear, and a lever to rotate the shift & select lever shaft). This actuator cannot be
disassembled.
(cid:2) The rotation of the shift motor is transmitted to the reduction gear and the shift & select lever shaft and
causes the shift & select inner lever to rotate.
(cid:2) The rotation of the select motor is transmitted to the rack & pinion gear and the shift & select lever shaft
and causes the shift & select inner lever to slide.
(cid:2) The movement of the shift & select inner lever is transmitted to the shimft fork shaft via the shift fork shaft
lever. As a result, the gear position changes.

Service Tip
Make sure to set the gear position to neutral before removing the shift & select actuator. In case that
the gear cannot be shifted to the neutral position due to malfunctions of the actuator and/or transaxle
gear, remove the plug from the transmission case and observe the position of the slit within. If the
slit is in a position other than neutral, use a screwdriver to set it to neutral.

CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION CH-19
Shift and Select Stroke Sensor
(cid:2) The shift and select stroke sensor consists of 2 Hall ICs and magnetic yoke that rotates in unison with
the shift & select lever shaft movement. The shift and select stroke sensors convent the changes in the
magnetic flux that are caused by the rotation of the shift motor and the select motor (hence, the rotation
of the magnetic yoke) into electric signals, and output them to the M-MT ECU. The M-MT ECU
determines the extent of the shift stroke and the select stroke from these electric signals in order to
determine the present gear position.
(cid:2) The main and sub circuits in the shift stroke sensor and the select stroke sensor exhibit the same output
characteristics.

CH-20 CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION
2. Clutch Actuator
General
(cid:2) The clutch actuator consists of clutch motor, clutch stroke sensor, worm shaft, worm wheel, pull rod*
and assist spring. This actuator cannot be disassembled.
(cid:2) The rotation of the clutch motor travels to the reduction gear of the worm shaft and the worm wheel, thus
causing the worm wheel to rotate. This movement causes the pull rod* to move via a fulcrum provided
on the worm wheel, actuating the clutch release fork.
(cid:2) The worm wheel is provided with an assist spring, which provides a force to assist the movement of the
pull rod* when the clutch is being operated. This reduces the load on the clutch motor at the time the
clutch is being released.
*: Only for the C53A multi-mode manual transmission. In the C551A multi-mode manual transmission,
this part is called push rod.

CHASSIS – MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION CH-21
Clutch Stroke Sensor
(cid:2) As same as the shift and select stroke sensors, the clutch stroke sensor consists primarily of 2 Hall ICs
(main and sub), and a magnetic yoke that rotates in unison with the rotation of the worm wheel.
(cid:2) The 2 Hall ICs convert the changes in the magnetic flux that are caused by the rotation of the clutch motor
(hence, the rotation of the magnetic yoke) into electric signals, and output them to the M-MT ECU. The
M-MT ECU determines the extent of the clutch stroke from these electric signals.
(cid:2) The main and sub circuits in the clutch stroke sensor exhibit the same output characteristics.
(cid:2) The basic construction and operation of the clutch stroke sensor is the same as those of the shift and select
stroke sensors.

CH-22 CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION
3. LCC (Load Controlled Clutch cover) Mechanism
General
(cid:2) To minimize the increase in the clutch operating load that results from the change in the pressure plate
height with the wear of the clutch disc facing, the LCC (Load Controlled Clutch cover) mechanism
mechanically adjusts the pressure plate height to a predetermined position.
(cid:2) The LCC mechanism is provided in the clutch cover and consists of a pinion and diaphragm spring which
are located on the upper pressure plate, and a lower rack, upper rack, adjust wedge, and spring which are
located on the lower pressure plate.
(cid:2) Upon determining an increase in the clutch operating load through the load (clutch motor amperage) that
is applied to the clutch motor, the M-MT ECU operates the clutch actuator in order to move the
diaphragm spring to the operating range of the LCC mechanism. This enables the LCC mechanism to
operate and mechanically adjust the pressure plate height to a predetermined position.

CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION CH-23
Operation
(cid:2)Upon determining an increase in the clutch operating load through the load (clutch motor amperage) that
is applied to the clutch motor, the M-MT ECU performs a clutch operation with an amount of clutch
release that is greater than normal*1, soon after the ignition switch is turned OFF with the vehicle
stopped. Thus, the diaphragm spring becomes more compressed than normal (LCC operation range),
allowing the upper pressure plate and the pinion to move more upward than normal. As a result, the pinion
and the upper rack become disengaged.
*1:The M-MT ECU starts this operation prior to the parking control (see page CH-35) that is performed
when the ignition switch is turned OFF.

(cid:2) When the pinion and the upper rack are disengaged, the spring force causes the upper rack to move
diagonally to the right along tihe teeth of the lower rack. As a result, the tooth position of the upper rack
d
and the pinion shifts by 1 pitch as compared to before the operation of the LCC mechanism.

CH-24 CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION
(cid:2) The M-MT ECU stops the clutch release operation and starts the clutch engagement operation. Because
the tooth position of the pinion and the lower rack is shifted 1 pitch as compared to before the operation
of the LCC mechanism, the pinion and the upper pressure plate move together to the left and engage with
the lower rack.
(cid:2) At this time, the upper pressure plate also moves to the left via the adjust wedge, causing the position
of the upper pressure plate to become higher than before the operation of the LCC mechanism. Due to
the rise in the upper pressure plate position, the diaphragm spring height decreases via the fulcrum. As
a result, the diaphragm spring becomes adjusted to a lower height than before the operation of the LCC
mechanism.

CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION CH-25
4. Shift Lever
General
(cid:2) The shift lever mainly consists of the shift lever position sensor, transmission shift main switch, shift lock

(cid:2) The multi-mode manual transmission system uses a shift-by-wire system in which the M-MT ECU
changes the gear position via actuators in accordance with the shift lever position that has been detected
through the shift lever position sensor and the transmission shift main switch.
(cid:2) An electrical shift lock mechanism that uses a shift lock solenoid to restrict the movement of the shift
lever is used.

CH-26 CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION
Shift Lever Position Sensor and Transmission Shift Main Switch
(cid:2) The shift lever position sensor consists of a Link 1 circuit that detects the R, N, E, and M positions of the shift
lever, and a Link 2 circuit that detects the “+” and “–” positions. The circuits are connected to the shift lever
via the respective links. The contact switches (6 contact switches for Link 1, and 2 contact switches for Link
2) of the circuits turn ON and OFF in accordance with the fore-aft movement of the shift lever. The M-MT
ECU determines the present shift lever position in accordance with the ON/OFF status of these contact
switches.
(cid:2) The transmission shift main switch detects the side-to-side movement of the shift lever. It turns OFF
when the shift lever is in the R, N, or E position, and ON in the M, “+”, or “–” position.

CH-30 CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION
3. Vehicle Starting Off Control
(cid:2) When the gear is in the 1st or 2nd position (shift lever in the E or M position) or reverse (shift lever in the
R position), and the brake pedal is released, the M-MT ECU partially engages the clutch via the clutch
actuator. To prevent engine stalls, the M-MT ECU requests the engine ECU to effect engine output control.
As a result, the vehicle can be driven slowly forward without requiring the driver to depress the accelerator
pedal. When the driver depresses the accelerator pedal in this condition, the M-MT ECU engages the
clutch.
(cid:2) When the parking brake lever is pulled up, the M-MT ECU releases the clutch with the clutch actuator,
and releases the vehicle starting off control.
(cid:2) If the partial engagement condition is prolonged, such as when driving on a hill, the M-MT ECU sounds
the built-in buzzer to alert the driver to avoid clutch damage.
(cid:2) If the shift lever is moved from the N to the E, M, or R position while the engine speed is high*, the M-MT
ECU sounds the built-in buzzer to alert the driver. At the same time, the M-MT ECU decreases the clutch
m
stroke speed (the speed of the engagement of the clutch) in order to prevent the vehicle from moving
suddenly.
*: The M-MT ECU determines this condition based on the transmissoion revolution sensor signal, crankshaft
position sensor signal, and accelerator pedal position sensor signal.

CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION CH-31
4. Shifting Control
General
The multi-mode manual transmission system consists of two types of shift modes: the E-mode that shifts
automatically to suit the driving conditions; and M-mode that allows the driver to manually operate the shift lever
to shift gears without applying the clutch. In the E-mode, the driver can select two shift patterns: the normal [E]
pattern (which is fuel economy-oriented and achieves fuel economy that surpasses that of general manual
transmission models) and the sporty [Es] pattern (which is drivability-oriented and enables sporty driving).
The M-MT ECU shifts gears by operating the clutch actuator and the shift & select actuator in accordance
with the shift lever position and the driving conditions. At the same time, it requests the engine ECU to effect
engine output control. As a result, smooth shifting control is realized.
(cid:2) When the shift lever is in the M position, the driver can operate the shift lever in the “+” or “–” direction
to enable the M-MT ECU to effect the gear shift control.
(cid:2) When the shift lever is in the E position, the M-MT ECU automatically selects an optimal gear position
m
that suits the driving conditions.

Downshifting (M-mode)
(cid:2) When the driver moves the shift lever from the M position to the “–” direction, the M-MT ECU operates
the clutch actuator to release the clutch.
(cid:2) In the M-mode, in order to achieve an excellent the driving usability and prevent engine stalls, the shifting
control shifts the gear down in accordance with the vehicle speed.
(cid:2) After the clutch release movement has been completed, the M-MT ECU operates the shift &select actuator
to shift gears. At this time, the M-MT ECU requests the engine ECU to effect engine output control.
(cid:2) After the gear shifting has been completed, the M-MT ECU engages the clutch.

Automatic Up/Downshifting (E-mode)
(cid:2) When the driver moves the shift lever to the E position, the M-MT ECU calculates an optimal gear
position to suit the driving conditions (accelerator pedal position, vehicle speed, etc.), and operates the
clutch actuator, shift & select actuator, and ETCS-i control or fuel injection volume control to shift the
gears (1st (cid:1) 5th). The control of the actuators, ETCS-i, fuel injection volume during upshifting and
downshifting is the same as for the M-mode.
(cid:2) The M-MT ECU determines the driver’s vehicle acceleration request from the accelerator pedal position
sensor value, and downshifts to a gear position that suits the vehicle speed if necessary. Thus, the vehicle
can be accelerated as requested by the driver even in the E-mode. In addition, the LHD models have the
kick-down function. This function enables the M-MT ECU to perform downshifting when the driver
fully depresses the accelerator pedal while driving, in accordance with the actual vehicle speed.

5. Shifting Control in Uphill/Downhill Traveling (E-mode)
General
This control minimizes the gear shifting when the driver operates the accelerator pedal while driving on a
winding uphill or downhill road in order to ensure a comfortable drivability.
Shift Control in Uphill Traveling
When the M-MT ECU determines uphill
traveling and before the transaxle attempts to
shift up or after it finishes to shift down
according to the control of shift timing, the
M-MT ECU prohibits the upshift until it
determines the uphill travel is over.

g

travel, it shifts down from 5th to 4th in
a
accordance with the brake operation signal that
is input when the driver operates the brake pedal.
i
When the M-MT ECU ddetermines downhill
travel with a steeper grade, and the brake
operation signal is inpurt again, the M-MT ECU
shifts down from a4th to 3rd, and the next time,
3rd to 2nd.

Uphill/Downhill Judgment
The actual acceleration calculated from the speed sensor signal is compared with the reference acceleration
stored in the M-MT ECU to judge uphill or downhill travel.

CH-34 CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION
6. (cid:2)TA (Delta Throttle Accelerate) Control
The (cid:2)TA control, which operates in the automatic or manual shift mode (E-mode or M-mode), has the
following two control functions:
(cid:2) At sudden acceleration: The M-MT ECU determines, via the accelerator pedal position sensor, a sudden
acceleration ON operation as the driver’s request to accelerate, and thus quickens the timing of
downshifting.
(cid:2) At sudden deceleration: The M-MT ECU determines, via the accelerator pedal position sensor, a sudden
acceleration OFF operation as the driver’s request to decelerate. Thus, instead of upshifting, it ensures
engine braking and also the driving force of the subsequent accelerator ON operation.
7. Vehicle Stopping Control
(cid:2) When the shift lever is in the E, M, or R position and the vehicle speed is decreased to a specific speed*,
the M-MT ECU releases the clutch to prevent engine stalls at low speeds.
m
(cid:2) In addition, when the vehicle is operating in the E or M position and comes to a stop, the gear is
automatically shifted to the 1st achieve an excellent drivability for starting off again. When the shift lever
o
is in the R position, the gear is not shifted automatically when the vehicle is stopped.
(cid:2) When the vehicle is stopped with its engine running and thec driver door is opened with the shift lever in
the E, M, or R position, the M-MT ECU sounds the buzzer in the combination meter to alert the driver.
.
*: Because the M-MT ECU effects this control in accorndance with the transmission revolution sensor signal,
this value changes with the gear position.

CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION CH-35
8. Parking Control
(cid:2) To park the vehicle, the driver applies the parking brake, moves the shift lever to the E, M or R position,
verifies on the shift position indicator light that the shifting has been properly executed, and turns the
ignition switch OFF. When the ignition switch is turned OFF, the shift lock mechanism causes the shift
lever to lock. Approximately 1 second later, the M-MT ECU engages the clutch. As a result, the transaxle
assumes the gear parking mode with its gears meshed.
(cid:2) The vehicle can be parked whether the shift lever is in any position, E, M, R, or N. However, when the
shift lever is in the N position, the gear parking mode described above cannot be effected because the gears
are not meshed. Also, when parking the vehicle using the gear parking mode, the driver must turn the
ignition switch OFF after the shift position indicator light is indicated “1” or “R”.
(cid:2) For approximately 5 seconds after the ignition switch has been turned OFF, the driver can verify the gear
position on the indicator due to the function of the meter ECU.

CH-38 CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION
11.Diagnosis
(cid:2)When the M-MT ECU detects a malfunction, the M-MT ECU makes a diagnosis and memorizes the failed
section. Furthermore, the M-MT ECU warning light and check engine warning light* illuminates or blinks
to inform the driver.
(cid:2)The M-MT ECU will also store the DTCs (Diagnostic Trouble Codes). The DTCs can be accessed by
connecting an intelligent tester II. They can also be accessed by connecting the SST (09843-18040) to the
DLC3 terminals Tc and CG, and reading the blinking of the M-MT warning light.
(cid:2)The DTCs cannot be deleted by merely disconnecting the battery terminals. To delete the DTCs, an
intelligent tester II must be connected to the DLC3 terminal or the SST (09843-18040) must be used to
initialize the M-MT ECU. For details on how to initialize the M-MT ECU, see page CH-40
For details, refer to the Yaris Repair Manual (Pub. No. RM00S0E).
*: Depending on the malfunctions, the check engine warning light illuminates.

prevent the vehicle’s drivability from being affected significantly. m

2. General Service Procedures
i
d
Clutch Position Adjustment
r
(cid:2) The clutch actuator can be adjusted to the clutch clamp position by effecting the clutch position
a
adjustment. This operation must be performed before removing the clutch actuator.
(cid:2) Other genceral service procedures (“clutch actuator preload” and “learning of multi-mode manual
transmission system”) are to be performed with the clutch actuator in the clutch clamp position.
Therefore, before performing an operation that involves a general service procedure, make sure to effect
the“clutch position adjustment”. If the “clutch position adjustment” is not effected, it will not be possible
to correctly perform the other general service procedures, which could result in a system malfunction.
(cid:2) Effect “clutch position adjustment” through one of the two ways described below:
– Use an intelligent tester II and connect it to the DLC3.
– Use the SST 09843-18040 and connect it to the Tc and CG terminals of the DLC3, and performed the
brake pedal and shift lever operation.
For details on the clutch position adjustment, see the Yaris Repair Manual (Pub. No. RM00S0E).

CH-40 CHASSIS– MULTI-MODE MANUAL TRANSMISSION
Adjustment and Initialization
(cid:2) Perform the 4 general service procedures (“clutch actuator preload”, “initialization of multi-mode
manual transmission ECU”, “learning of multi-mode manual transmission system” and
“synchronization position calibration”) upon the completion of the removal and reinstallation of any part
related to the multi-mode manual transmission system.
(cid:2) For details on the general service procedures, see the Yaris Repair Manual (Pub. No. RM00S0E).

release fork without preload, to fasten three bolts to make sure that the
clutch actuator should be adjusted to the clutch clamp position.

The learning of the M-MT system can be performed through one of
the two ways described below:

b)Turn the ignition switch from OFF to ON, stand by for 40 seconds,

U340E AND U441E AUTOMATIC TRANSAXLES
(cid:1)DESCRIPTION
(cid:2) The new model uses 2 types (U340E, U441E) of automatic transaxle.
(cid:2) A U340E automatic transaxle is provided on the 1NZ-FE engine model, and a U441E automatic transaxle
is provided on the 2NZ-FE engine model.
(cid:2) A low-viscosity type automatic transmission fluid (Toyota Genuine ATF WS) is used for the U340E
automatic transaxle.
(cid:2) Shift control in uphill/downhill traveling is used for the automatic transaxles.
(cid:2) The basic construction of the automatic transaxles is the same as on the previous model.

CHASSIS– U340E AND U441E AUTOMATIC TRANSAXLES CH-43
(cid:1)ATF (AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSION FLUID) WS
(cid:2) ATF WS is used in the U340E automatic transaxle.
(cid:2) The ATF WS is used to reduce the resistance of the ATF and ensure fuel economy by reducing its viscosity
in the practical operation range. At the high-temperature end, its viscosity is the same as that of the ATF
Type T-IV, which ensures the durability of the automatic transaxle.
(cid:2) There is no interchangeability between the ATF WS and other types of ATFs (ATF Type T-IV, D-II).

CH-54 CHASSIS– U340E AND U441E AUTOMATIC TRANSAXLES
3. Centrifugal Fluid Pressure Canceling Mechanism
There are two reasons for improving the conventional clutch mechanism:
(cid:2) To prevent the generation of pressure by the centrifugal force that applied to the fluid in piston fluid
pressure chamber (hereafter referred to as “chamber A”) when the clutch is released, a check ball is
provided to discharge the fluid. Therefore, before the clutch can be subsequently applied, it took time for
the fluid to fill the chamber A.
(cid:2) During shifting, in addition to the original clutch pressure that is controlled by the valve body, the pressure
that acts on the fluid in the chamber A also exerts influence, which is dependent upon revolution
fluctuations.
To address these two needs for improvement, a canceling fluid pressure chamber (hereafter referred to as
“chamber B”) has been provided opposite chamber A.

o

.

g
a

U340E Automatic Transaxle
d
By utilizing the lubrication fluid such as that of the shaft, the same amount of centrifugal force is applied,
r
thus canceling the centrifugal force that is applied to the piston itself. Accordingly, it is not necessary to
a
discharge the fluid through the use of a check ball, and a highly responsive and smooth shifting characteristic
has been achieved. c
