EG-188 ENGINE– 1ND-TV ENGINE
2) For Model with Smart Entry & Start System
(cid:3) Once the engine switch is pushed, this function continues to operate the starter until the engine has
started, provided that the brake pedal on the M-MT models or clutch pedal on the M/T models is
pressed. This prevents starting failures and the engine from being cranked after the engine has started.
(cid:3) When the engine ECU detects a start signal from the main body ECU, this system monitors the engine
speed (NE) signal and continues to operate the starter until it has determined that the engine has started.
Furthermore, even if the engine ECU detects a starter signal from the main body ECU, this system
will not operate if the engine ECU has determined that the engine has already started.

ENGINE– 1ND-TV ENGINE EG-189
Operation
1) For Models without Smart Entry & Start System
(cid:3) As indicated in the following timing chart, when the engine ECU detects a start signal from the ignition
switch, it energizes the starter relay to operate the starter. If the engine is already running, the engine
ECU will not energize the starter relay.
(cid:3) After the starter operates and the engine speed becomes higher than approximately 500 rpm, the
engine ECU determines that the engine has started and stops the operation of the starter.
(cid:3) If the engine has any failure and does not work, the starter operates as long as its maximum continuous
operation time and stops automatically. The maximum continuous operation time is approximately
2 seconds through 25 seconds depending on the water temperature condition. When the engine water EG
temperature is extremely low, it is approximately 25 seconds and when the engine is warmed up
sufficiently, it is approximately 2 seconds.
(cid:3) This system cuts off the current that powers the accessoriems while the engine is cranking to prevent
the accessory illumination from operating intermittently due to the unstable voltage that is associated
with the cranking of the engine.
o
(cid:3) This system has the following protections.
c
– On the M-MT models, while the engine is running normally, even if turning the ignition switch to
START position, the starter will not operat.e.
n
– On the M-MT models, in case that the driver keeps holding the ignition switch in START position
and the engine starts half way, the engine ECU will stop the starter operation when the engine speed
g
has becomes between 900 and 1,000 rpm, which varies according to the water temperature.
– On the M-MT models, in caase that the driver keeps holding the ignition switch in START position and
the engine will not start, the engine ECU will stop the starter operation when approximately 30 seconds
i
have been passed. d
– In case that the starter begins to operate, but cannot detect the engine speed signal, the engine ECU
r
will stop the starter operation immediately.
a

EG-190 ENGINE– 1ND-TV ENGINE
2) For Models with Smart Entry & Start System Model
(cid:3) As indicated in the following timing chart, when the engine ECU detects a start signal (STSW) from
the main body ECU, the engine ECU outputs STAR and ACCR signals to the main body ECU at the
same time that the engine preheating indicator light turns off. Upon detecting the STAR and ACCR
signals from the engine ECU, the main body ECU energizes the starter relay to operate the starter. If
the engine running, the engine ECU stops the output of the STAR and ACCR signals to the main body
ECU. Thus, the main body ECU will not energize the starter relay.
However, if the engine ECU detects a start signal again while the engine preheating indicator light is
on, the engine ECU outputs STAR and ACCR signals and main body ECU energizes the starter relay
to operate the starter.
(cid:3) After the starter operates and the engine speed becomes higher than approximately 500 rpm, the
engine ECU determines that the engine has started and stops the output of the STAR and ACCR signals
to the main body ECU. Thus, the main body ECU will stop the operation of the starter.
(cid:3) If the engine has any failure and does not work, the starter operates as long as its maximum continuous
operation time and stops automatically. The maximum continuous operation time is approximately
2 seconds through 25 seconds depending on the water temperature condition. When the engine water
temperature is extremely low, it is approximately 25 seconds anmd when the engine is warmed up
sufficiently, it is approximately 2 seconds.
(cid:3) This system cuts off the current that powers the accessories while the engine is cranking to prevent
o
the accessory illumination from operating intermittently due to the unstable voltage that is associated
with the cranking of the engine.
c
(cid:3) This system has following protections.
.
– While the engine is running normally, the starter does not operate.
n
– In case the driver keeps pressing the engine switch and the engine starts half way, the engine ECU
stops the output of the STAR and ACgCR signals when the engine speed becomes higher than
between 900 and 1,000 rpm, which varies according to the water temperature. Thus, the main body
ECU will stop the operation of thae starter.
– In case the driver keeps pressing the engine switch and the engine does not start, the engine ECU
i
stops the output of the STAR and ACCR signals after 30 seconds have elapsed. Thus, the main body
d
ECU will stop the operation of the starter.
– In case the engine ECrU cannot detect an engine speed signal while the starter is operating, the
engine ECU will iammediately stop the output of the STAR and ACCR signals. Thus, the main body
ECU will stop the operation of the starter.
c

ENGINE– 1ND-TV ENGINE EG-191
15. Oil Maintenance Management System
General
(cid:3) An oil maintenance management system is newly used. This system determines the deterioration
conditions of the engine oil and illuminates an oil change reminder light to inform the driver when the
engine oil and oil filter must be changed. Accordingly, the maintenance intervals (40,000 km maximum)
that correspond to the actual deterioration conditions of the engine oil have been realized.
(cid:3) This system indirectly determines the deterioration of the engine oil based on the information provided
by the engine ECU.

EG-192 ENGINE– 1ND-TV ENGINE
System Operation
(cid:3) This system determines the deterioration of the engine oil in accordance with the soot volume in the
engine oil. The engine ECU calculates the soot volume in the engine oil in accordance with engine speed,
injection timing, injection volume, and intake air mass. The engine ECU blinks the oil change reminder
light when the calculated value of the soot volume approaches the predetermined value, and illuminates
the light when the calculated value exceeds the predetermined value. Thus, this system informs the driver
that the engine oil and oil filter must be changed.

(cid:3) In addition to controlling the blinking and illumination of the oil change reminder light by counting the
n
soot volume, the engine ECU blinks the oil change reminder light when the vehicle’s driven distance
exceeds 35,000 km, and illuminates the lightg when the distance exceeds 40,000 km. Thus, this function
realizes the excellent reliability of the system.
a
NOTICE:
i
d
This system does not determine the deterioration of the engine oil based on the elapsed time. Even
if the oil change reminder light does not illuminate, the engine oil and oil filter should be changed
r
at 2-year intervals at the maximum.
a
Counter Reset c
The accumulated mileage/kilometrage memorized in the engine ECU can be reset by the following
procedures.
For Model without Smart Entry & Start System
(cid:3) Turn the ignition key to the IG-ON position.
(cid:3) Check that the LCD display screen has changed to the ODO mode.
(The reset procedure will not function in TRIP mode.)
(cid:3) Turn the ignition key to the OFF position.
(cid:3) While pressing the ODO/TRIP switch, turn the ignition key to the IG-ON position. Do not depress
the clutch pedal or brake pedal during this operation. After turning the ignition key to the IG-ON
position, press and hold the ODO/TRIP switch for 5 seconds or more. When the LCD display screen
shows no display, release the ODO/TRIP switch.
(cid:3) The oil change reminder light goes off and then the LCD display indicates “000000” for about 1 sec.
(cid:3) The LCD display should change to the ODO mode.
(cid:3) This completes the reset procedure.

ENGINE– 1ND-TV ENGINE EG-193
For Model with Smart Entry & Start System
(cid:3) Push the engine switch and set the start system to the IG-ON mode.
(cid:3) Make sure that the LCD display screen is the ODO mode.
(Reset procedure will not work in the TRIP mode)
(cid:3) Push the engine switch and set the start system to the OFF mode.
(cid:3) While pressing the ODO/TRIP switch, push the engine switch to set the system to the IG-ON mode
without depressing the clutch pedal. After setting the system to the IG-ON mode, hold down the
ODO/TRIP switch for more than 5 seconds. When the LCD display screen shows no display, release
the ODO/TRIP switch.
(cid:3) The oil change reminder light goes off and then the LCD display indicates “000000” for about 1 sec.
EG
(cid:3) The LCD display should change to the ODO mode.
(cid:3) This completes the reset procedure.
m
16. Diagnosis
(cid:3) The diagnosis system of the 1ND-TV engine uses the E o URO-OBD (Europe On-Board Diagnosis).
(cid:3) When the engine ECU detects a malfunction, the ecngine ECU makes a diagnosis of the failed section.
Furthermore, the check engine warning light in the combination meter illuminates or blinks to inform
.
the driver.
n
(cid:3) By using the intelligent tester II, the 5-digit DTCs (Diagnostic Trouble Codes) and engine ECU data can
be read out. Moreover, the ACTIVE TgEST can be used to drive the actuator by means of the intelligent
tester II.
a
(cid:3) The engine ECU can output freeze-frame data to the intelligent tester II. This data is stored in the engine
ECU at the very moment whein the engine ECU has detected its last data of malfunction.
d
(cid:3) All the DTCs have been made to correspond to the SAE Controlled codes. Some of the DTCs have been
further divided into srmall detection areas, and new DTCs have been assigned to them. For details, refer
to the Yaris Repair Manual (Pub. No. RM00S0E).
a
c
Service Tip
To clear the DTC that is stored in the engine ECU, use the intelligent tester II, disconnect the battery
terminal or remove the EFI fuse for 1 minute or longer.
17. Fail-Safe
When a malfunction is detected by any of the sensors, there is a possibility of an engine or another
malfunction occurring if the engine ECU were to continue to control the engine control system in the normal
way. To prevent such a problem, the fail-safe function of the engine ECU either relies on the data stored
in memory to allow the engine control system to continue operating, or stops the engine if a hazard is
anticipated. For details, refer to the Yaris Repair Manual (Pub. No. RM00S0E).

NEW MODEL OUTLINE MO-23
EQUIPMENT
Smart Entry & Start System
Smart entry & start system mainly consists of the wireless door lock remote control function, engine
immobilizer function, start function and entry function.
MO
This system is available as optional equipment on the SOL grade for the Europe models.
Start Function
In the start function, the power source mode can be switched between OFF, ACC, and IG-ON by pushing
the engine switch when the driver carries the key.
In addition, when the driver carries the key the driver can start the engine by pushing the engine switch while
depressing the brake pedal (the multi-mode manual transmissionm models) or the clutch pedal (the manual
transaxle models).

MO-24 NEW MODEL OUTLINE
Entry Function
In the entry function, the following functions can be operated when the driver carries the key:
(cid:1) The engine switch illumination and room lights come on if the driver gets close to the vehicle. (Entry
Illumination)
(cid:1) The doors unlock if the door outside handle is gripped. (Entry Unlock)
(cid:1) The doors lock if the lock switch on the outside door handle is pushed. (Entry Lock)
(cid:1) The back door opens if the back door open switch is pushed. (Entry Back Door Open)

NOTICE:
The construction of the spray type washer nozzle does not allow adjustment of the spray angle.
Do not adjust the spray angle as this could damage the washer nozzle.

MO-34 NEW MODEL OUTLINE
SAFETY
SRS Airbag System
(cid:1)SRS (Supplemental Restraint System) airbags are standard equipment for the driver and front passenger
seats. In the event of a severe frontal collision, the airbags function with the seat belts to help reduce the
impact on the driver’s and front passenger’s head and chest.
(cid:1)SRS knee airbag is used for the driver seat. The SRS knee airbag is positioned in the lower part of the
instrument panel on driver’s side to help ensure good protection of the driver in the event of a frontal
collision.
(cid:1)The SRS side airbags (driver and front passenger seats) which help reduce side impacts are used.
(cid:1)The SRS curtain shield airbags (front and rear seats) which help reduce side impacts are used.
(cid:1)The provision of SRS airbags, SRS knee airbags, SRS side airbags and SRS curtain shield airbags vary
m
by destinations and grades. For details, see the Equipment List starting on page MO-37 or the SRS Airbag
System on page BE-99.
